28The eight mental dregs of an unfortunate person.\n1. {nyon} {mongs pa'i 'ching ba dam po yod pa/} he or she is strongly bound by delusions\n2. {kun spyod shin tu ngan pa/} is extremely bad mannered\n3. {'khor ba la yid mi skyo ba/} has no remorse about living in [saṁsāra]\n4. {dad pa cung zad kyang med pa/} has total lack of faith in Dharma\n5. {mi dge'i sdig pa spyod pa/} commits non-virtues\n6. {chos la mi sems pa/} has no interest in the Dharma\n7. {sdom pa nyams pa/} has lost his or her vows\n8. {dam tshig 'chal ba/} has corrupted the samaya or commitment.

2Jetsunma. reverend lady, nun (h); Title used for a female reverend; female practitioner8lady of certain religious group, charitable or devout woman91) principal/ chief lady; 2) [noble] nun reverend lady [h]; 3) goddess; 4) Jetsunma ([IW]261) {gtso mo'am bdag mo/} 2) {sku drag rigs kyi rab byung ma/} 3) {lha mo/}36<person> Bhaṭṭā, Bhaṭṭārikā37<person> Bhaṭṭā (Skt: bhaṭṭā): A yakṣiṇī invoked in magical rites. (Original glossary entry: https://84000.co/glossary/entity-11199.html)37<person> Bhaṭṭārikā (Skt: bhaṭṭārikā): An epithet meaning “noble lady” commonly associated with the goddess Tārā in Buddhist literature, it is also applied to other goddesses in the Buddhist and Hindu pantheons. In The Maṇḍala Rites of Noble Mārīcī, the term appears as either an epithet of the goddess Durgā or perhaps Mārīcī. (Original glossary entry: https://84000.co/glossary/entity-44624.html)39<person> bhaṭṭā, bhaṭṭārikā421) devī 2) bhaṭṭārikā 3) vajravārāhī431. bhaṭṭārikā {rje btsun ma 'phags ma sgrol ma} bhaṭṭārikāryatārā ka.ta.2327 2. nā. bhaṭṭā, yakṣiṇī — bhaṭṭāyā mantraḥ OM bhaṭṭe bhaṭṭe ālokini kiṃ cirāyasi ? ehyehi āgacchāgaccha \n mama kāryaṃ kuru svāhā ma.mū.283ka/441.471. {sku drag rigs kyi rab byung ma/}472. {lha mo/}53{lha mo'i mtshan/ tshig 'di gsar du bcos pa yin/}53{sku drag rigs kyi a Ni la zer/ tshig 'di gsar du bcos pa yin/}55(1) {gtso mo'am bdag mo/} (2) {sku drag rigs kyi rab byung ma/} (3) {lha mo/}

55{dge lugs pa'i grub mtha'i srol gtod pa po rje tsong kha pa chen po mchog ste cung zhib tsam gsham du tsa sde'i nang tsong kha pa zhes par gzigs/}
21357-14198tsong khapa, name of founder of the Gelugpa sect28Tsong Khapa (1357-1419). The founder of the Gelug order of Tibetan Buddhism known for his revival of the Kadampa tradition. He also elucidated the most subtle meanings of [sūtra] and tantra in innumerable discourses and his collected works comprise 18 volumes. His teachings and those of his two maln disciples Gyaltsab-Je and Khedrub-Je remain in the heart of the Gelug tradition.

mtshan mo mar me'i phreng ba mtha' yas pa phul ba rnams dang / gzims khang gi sgo drung dang phyi rol gyi chos gra'i zhal dkar thad so rnams su mar me'i phreng ba tshar du dang ra ba thams cad kyang mtshan ring gang la me lce'i rtse mo cung zad rlung gis g.yo ba tsam yang med par gyur to/ /gzhan yang lha rnams kyis mngon par mchod pa dang / dpa' bo dang mkha' 'gro ma rnams kyis mngon par bsgrags pa'i rol mo'i bye brag bsam gyis mi khyab pa mtshan gung la grags pa dang / nyin mo yang sngon tshor ma myong ba'i sgra'i bye brag du ma 'ga' zhig gi rna lam na grags pa la sogs pa dang / lha rnams kyis mngon par gtor ba'i me tog gi tshogs ni phal che ba kun+da ltar dkar zhing mu tig ltar 'od gsal bas mthon po nas 'byung ba na zla ba ltar 'tsher ba dang / 'ga' zhig ni kha dog sngon po dang / 'ga' zhig ni ser po dang / 'ga' zhig ni dmar po la sogs pa mi chen po'i lag pa'i mthil tsam pa dang / shing rta'i phang lo tsam pa dpag tu med pa char ltar du bab par gyur to/ /gzhan yang phal che bar tshogs pas lda ldi ltar khrig gis gang bar gyur pa la sogs pa zhag mang po'i bar du byung zhing / gang gi tshe bcom ldan 'das grong khyer rtswa can du gzims mal gyi tha ma la gzims te mya ngan las 'da' ba'i mdzad pa bstan pa na lha rnams kyis mngon par mchod pa ji lta ba de ltar gyur to/ /zhes gsungs so/ /} (1357-1419)

8demon that takes away the power of memory, epilepsy36<term> apasmāra37<term> apasmāra (Skt: apasmāra): A class of nonhuman beings believed to cause epilepsy, fits, and loss of memory. As their name suggests—the Skt. apasmāra literally means “without memory” and the Tib. brjed byed means “causing forgetfulness”—they are defined by the condition they cause in affected humans, and the term can refer to any nonhuman being that causes such conditions, whether a bhūta, a piśāca, or other.
